However, over the last 15, 20 years, we have had to realize that there is also a gap between low incomes on the one hand and high incomes on the other. The family bonus – I have already done this before – will further promote this shear development. One thing is also important to me in this context: With regard to the family bonus, it is always the talk of being created for those people who pay taxes – that is commonly said –; it hurts me when I hear that. These are, of course, the people who pay wage and income taxes. However, if we look at the overall tax burden, we can see that the low incomes have almost the same tax burden as the highest incomes. The lower income third pays almost the same amount of taxes and taxes as the upper income third: the lower 43 percent, the upper 45 percent. That means nothing but that in Austria we have a tax and tax system that is not progressive but very close to a flat tax. Why is that so? – This is because, although the lower incomes do not pay wage and income taxes, all other excise duties and social security contributions, the lower incomes are relatively higher than the higher incomes. This also means, on the other hand, that all taxpayers contribute to the financing of the welfare state in our country. That is why I feel this debate, which is being held here, and also the family bonus as something very dishonest. It would be much more honest to say: Yes, the tax burden, as it is, is unfair. And so it would be more honest to say: We do not relieve ourselves of a family bonus – with all the disadvantages I have mentioned earlier, namely that it is dependent on income and therefore lower incomes are disadvantaged and favoured above – but we do make a completely different solution, which all favour the same. You can do this in different ways. This can be done, for example, by increasing child support. This would not be the desired side effect that the government would like to have, namely that it lowers the tax and tax rate. Of course, one can also reorganise the family bonus in a way that it is very likely to reduce the tax and tax rate, but is also fair from a distribution policy perspective. In this context, I would therefore like to make the following motion for a resolution: [[Quoted print version removed]] How can I justify that? . The text of the resolution is actually quite clear. What do we need to do? – We simply need a negative-taxing base down and a limiting base up. This would be a fair family bonus or a fair family benefit, which is independent of income and would actually create more tax justice than the family bonus is currently doing. Thank you. Klaus Uwe Feichtinger.]] I do not complain, but I want to point out that years ago we started to expand this disaster prevention. Nearly after the war, the Austrian hail insurance was founded to protect against hail damage. Over the years and decades, however, we have expanded this disaster prevention in the agricultural sector and developed so-called multi-risk insurance, so that farmers can not only insure themselves against hail damage, but also against other dangers. For example, in 1995 there was a large package with which hail insurance was extended towards frost insurance, towards flood and storm, everything that could affect crops. But it was further expanded: In 2000, there was a first drought insurance, which generally insured against precipitation deficits. In 2003, insurance in the field of animal husbandry increased – this can also happen. In 2006 there was a frost insurance in the wine-growing industry for the first time, 2015 a drought index insurance for Greenland, a frost insurance at Kernobst as well as an animal health insurance, 2016 a drought index insurance for maize, 2017 a drought index insurance for wheat and sugar beet, up to frost insurance at Steinobst. Why am I counting that? – We have consistently expanded the insurance system and, above all, we have funded the premiums by the federal government and also by the federal states, because the insurance premiums were so expensive for many farmers that they have not been insured. When it came to a frost or a hail, the reputation for the disaster fund became loud. We have chosen the strategy that the state, namely the federal government and the country, should grant the insurance premium, thus making the insurance available to the farmer; and when the disaster occurs, the insurance will be hit. In this sense, it was important to do this and continue to do so. That's all we need to do. An important step has been taken with the harmonization of the insurance tax here today. Just one number: This year there have been disaster damage in agriculture and forestry to some EUR 110 million, with approximately EUR 250 million in the previous year. The year is not yet over, and unfortunately something can still happen; therefore it was important to do that. Thank you to the Minister of Finance, as well as to the Minister of Agriculture for the willingness to send these important signals to agriculture and forestry, which, despite all technology and all digitization, which also use in agriculture and forestry, is still very much dependent on wind and weather! I would also like to make an amendment to the Annual Tax Act; I would like to explain it in the key points: On the one hand, we are talking about a statement that auditors and tax advisors both make opinions and issue confirmations. These terms are clarified here. The second point is that the term "sachwalter" is replaced by "adult representative" or that the term "precautionary agent" is also introduced. Finally, the financial institutions are obliged to allow this implementation. To give it time, it's supposed to be 1. January 2019 will be staggering. I believe that the amendment has been accepted by the political groups. Thank you very much. Annual tax laws create clarity and legal certainty for taxpayers, legal certainty means planning security; we all know that planning security is also an important location factor. In terms of content, the Annual Tax Act also provides for greater legal certainty. The notice of information is thus a contribution to greater legal certainty. The Annual Tax Act extends the possibility of binding information on matters not yet implemented in accordance with the Government's programme to other topics, namely international tax law and sales tax. In addition, it will be possible to obtain legal certainty in the future by means of a notice of information as to whether or not a intended design is to be regarded as an abuse. That means there is also greater legal certainty for entrepreneurs. Another point of legal certainty: the accompanying control vulgo horizontal monitoring. The accompanying control is a voluntary – I emphasize: voluntary – alternative to the classical external examination. In the case of participating entrepreneurs, an internal tax control system, developed by the entrepreneur himself and audited by a tax advisor or auditor, in conjunction with an extended disclosure obligation and ongoing contact with the tax authority shall replace the subsequent external audit. What now is this extended disclosure obligation? – Participants shall already disclose facts before the tax declaration is issued if there is a serious risk that the financial management may decelegate the facts. ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e8346	The next item is the report by Mr Deputies Head. - Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e8368	Madam President. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) It won't surprise you if I disagree with my colleagues Krainer. I think that with this bill we have found a very good, balanced arrangement, namely between the contrary interests of insurance companies on the one hand and insured persons on the other, a good compromise taking into account the European legal situation. We have also spent a lot of time on this balance of interests and the discussion in this regard. What's this about? – In the past, there has been a situation where the legal basis, the deadline for withdrawal after the conclusion of an insurance contract has changed several times and insurances have failed to understand in their own documents the conditions of insurance. And this has led to the fact that the case-law, up to the European Court of Justice, has established the nullity of such contracts or the permanent possibility of withdrawal, which has been established for a lifetime of withdrawal. Quite frankly: While it is not to be criticised for court judgments, I believe that this is a completely outrageous assessment, namely, that insured persons are at first due to this mistake – it is more than a formal error; “Bringing errors” is called in the legal jargon – life-long, yes, even after payment of life insurance, which may not have been so desirable in its economic performance, can also say retroactively the withdrawal from the insurance, with repayment of all premiums including a juicy interest rate. That can't be right! This is due to the judgment of the Court of Justice, but I am sure that I do not feel that it is not, nor does it, after the legal sense of many, at least the majority in the committee and here in the house. What have we done now? – On the basis of this ECJ legal statement, which I believe has initially interpreted the German OGH and then the Austrian overrun, insured can continue to have the same consequences – in my opinion overwhelming consequences, but of course, legal certainty – out of old contracts, with repayment of all premiums including interest; but from 1st century onwards. January next year will be subject to other terms of withdrawal, i.e. upon withdrawal in the first year following the conclusion of the contract also repayment of all premiums and after the first year up to five years repayment, but then without accounting for the mediation costs. I think that this is a good compromise, because it was important to ensure that, on the one hand, capital-building life assurances, this important instrument for future security, from an economic point of view, do not become uninteresting for insurance, so that they may no longer be able to offer them at all, and, on the other hand, of course, fairness for the insured is given that they have, of course, exit possibilities, without any reason in the first year, and therefore no economic advantage from it. This is the first time that we have been able to find a solution to this problem. Of course, this cannot apply for life as a whole, because the last end of this ad absurd instrument would lead to ad-absurdum. In short: a little complicated, but at the end of the day, I think, a clean, fair solution, taking into account the interests of insurance companies on the one hand, connected since the interests of intermediaries, i.e. brokers and insurance agents, and the interests of the insured, consumers, on the other hand. It is a fair solution and a fair compromise for all. I ask you to give your consent.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e9041	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! The double taxation agreement between Austria and the Russian Federation dates back to 2000. With the present Protocol, we implement, on the one hand, OECD's principles of transparency and administrative assistance in tax matters, and on the other hand, if real estate is sold in accordance with OECD standards in the future, it will also be possible to pay the income in the issuing State. What is quite important to me in this agreement is the unilateral declaration of Austria that, in accordance with UN resolution 68/262, we are not going to see the Crimea and Sevastopol – parts of Ukraine occupied by Russia – included in the double taxation agreement, but to exclude these parts, because we do not want to interfere with the territorial integrity of Ukraine in any way and, of course, also follow the United Nations in this respect. The renewed double taxation agreement also includes an exchange of information in the OECD State of the Art, i.e. information on tax matters which are not subject to confidentiality or confidentiality provisions, must be provided by the other country, even if it has no interest in such information itself. There must be no refusal to provide information and mutual assistance for the enforcement of tax documents is also agreed. I would also like to use this item on the agenda as an opportunity to point out that, as the Republic of Austria, we also have a legally securitised coherence offer; that is, all our policies, all our legislative measures, must also be used to help a global development. This is not relevant in the case of the double taxation agreement with the Russian Federation, but in the case of other double taxation agreements it is quite the same that one must also look at how these affect the partner countries. Especially when it comes to talking about international tax policy, the Domestic Resource Mobility is always an important crux to the fact that countries of the global south also have the opportunity to respond accordingly. To achieve tax revenue in order to carry out its tasks, such as the development of social infrastructure, i.e. expenditure on education, health, public transport and others. It is very important to me that, in the case of double taxation agreements with countries of the global south, we should really pursue this coherence in the future and look forward to the fact that poorer countries also have their share of tax income in order to have the opportunity to meet their social and economic development commitments. – Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e9086	The next item is the speech by Mr Hanger. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e9108	Madam President. Federal Minister! Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! My contribution to the debate refers to item 3 and 4. This is essentially a change in the Capital Market Act or the Alternative Financing Act. This is a bulky title, but actually a very exciting topic. The issue is risk capital financing, and the issue is therefore exciting because risk capital can create jobs with appropriate framework conditions. Of all the policy-making tasks, job creation is certainly one of the most important. In 2015, we created the legal framework for alternative financing, for crowdfunding. In terms of terms, it is about crowdinvesting – one participates with small deposits in a company – or crowdling – you give almost in small amounts money for a company. Why is that important? – If a young person or an Austrian has a business idea, what do you need? You need a vision, you need a concept, you ideally need a well established, well thought-out business plan, but at the end of the day you also need funding. There are several possibilities. Ideally, you have the necessary equity; this will be the case for young people in the rarest cases. The second option is to go to a bank to organise foreign capital; we know since the 2008/2009 financial crisis, that has become more difficult, the banking market, the foreign capital market has been highly regulated – Basel III, Basel IV are the key words – and you need appropriate collateral. Unfortunately, it happens very often in Austria that then a very, very good business idea, which is there, cannot be implemented because the necessary funding is missing, and then the Alternative Financing Act takes effect. About Crowdinvesting, crowdfunding, via platforms inviting the crowd to finance a business idea in small amounts, is a very, very good idea. However, what has the balance sheet been like since 2015? - I looked at this a little bit. In 2017, crowdfunding, according to crowdfunding platform CrowdCircus, financed 28.8 million euros. If you look at the total financial volume, you have to say that this is a very, very small amount. Although this amount has increased by 48 percent, the economic impact of the economy has not yet been fully assessed. What can this be about? – Among other things, this is certainly due to the fact that there may not really be an appropriate culture for risk capital; perhaps it is the instrument is too little known, but it is certainly also because this market is so strictly regulated. We have created too strict regulations as if this instrument were relevant for start-ups, for example, and that is precisely where the novelle of the Alternative Financing Act and the Capital Market Act is in place. One reduces the information obligations which are a major bureaucratic obstacle to businesses. There will then be no more information requirements up to 250 000 euros. Simplification of information requirements for amounts between EUR 250 000 and EUR 5 million. From EUR 5 million funding volume, the capital market prospectus remains, of course, the same as it has been so far. It remains unchanged – and I would also like to mention this – that EUR 5 000 is the maximum amount for a private investor that can be invested in a start-up, of course, divided into several. So this provision of protection continues to apply, and I believe that we can also discuss it. At the end of the day, the statement remains that risk capital will always remain risk capital. Everyone must be aware of this; but there is a value that is very important to us, which is: Self-reliance. We must also give people the freedom to act on their own responsibility, take back regulations, and the alternative funding instrument will also become more important to us in Austria. This creates jobs again, and I think that's exactly what we want. – Thank you very much for the attention.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e9208	The next item is the speech by Mr Klinger. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e9234	Madam President. Mr Finanzminister! Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen. Listeners and listeners! So I am not quite sure whether a social debate is properly placed under item 6 of the agenda, because it is a matter of promoting the development of economic relations between Austria and Russia, and above all, it is also about adjusting the protocols according to OECD standards. A short chronology on this: The existing double taxation agreement with Russia, which is to be amended today, has already been signed in 2000. A change occurred in December 2009 in Vienna, and in June 2011 a negotiation took place in Moscow. That is, this amendment, this adaptation has already taken a long time, and I believe that it is time and also in the sense of our government coalition to make this agreement properly, just as we want to deal with all of these economic problems that are going to be dealt with at a rapid pace. The results of the OECD's and G 20 Action Plans to prevent the reduction of profits and the relocation of profits have already been achieved in this Agreement. Background information on this is that the Agreement adds a provision concerning the limits of the grant of the Agreement: ‘The benefit shall be refused provided that the main purpose or one of the main purposes of the taxable person or a person associated with him was to receive the benefit. The provision is based on the ‘Action Points 6’ developed by the OECD within the framework of the BEPS’ – which is the prevention of the reduction of profits and the shift in profits – ‘PPT’ (principal purpose test). The new OECD standard on fiscal transparency and willingness to provide assistance is implemented by amending the provision on the exchange of information – and that is very important – as well as the introduction of administrative assistance in the implementation. The current provision of the exchange of information does not meet the requirements of the OECD and the current OECD pattern agreement, so the determination is adapted to the new model agreement. The previous DBA provisions with Russia for enforcement aid were completely non-existent. These too are now covered by this agreement. In addition, some changes have been made to the provisions on the distribution of tax rights in the case of passive income. The dividend article of the Agreement was simplified at the suggestion of the economy. It is important that the protocol is the first agreement with the Russian Federation since the Crimean crisis in 2014. Therefore, a declaration on the territorial scope of this Agreement was also necessary. This declaration of Austria corresponds exactly to the statement of territorial scope already made in 2014 between the EU and the Russian Federation. The Ministry of Finance has no special financial impact. The main advantages are legal certainty and adaptation to OECD requirements.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e11500	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! High house! Dear ladies and gentlemen at home in front of the screens! Today, we are discussing the Second Federal Law Enforcement Act, which brings many benefits to legal users in particular. Almost 19 years ago, the Hohe Haus had already passed a First Federal Law Clearing Act in a similar form. At that time, some 250 out of 500 standards were affected, and about 170 other legislation was repealed by the 2006 Deregulation Act. The legislative project which is now under way is the most extensive step towards the shattering of the Austrian legal system, and that is also good, ladies and gentlemen. There should again be a good overview of our laws, and at a glance people should know what is going on and what is not. Right must be understandable in order to be able to use it, is a very important currency. And this does not apply to the language of the respective laws, but to their classification. The flood of law over the last decades poses a danger that the rule of law may suffocate in the fullness of its own laws. It should not be our goal to make life more difficult for people, but we want to make people's lives easier. And when bureaucracy and regulation rise, there is less and less freedom and personal responsibility for people in this country, ladies and gentlemen. We want to simplify the lives of citizens, also simplify the lives of the authorities and businesses, and strengthen freedom and self-reliance. This law, ladies and gentlemen, is intended to dissociate unconsisting legislation in order to increase legal certainty and provide the basis for further reform. Dead law should no longer necessarily be part of the Austrian legal system. I'll give you a few examples to illustrate this practically a little: Do we need a regulation that allows an exception to the weekend rest during the 1991 Alpine Ski Championship? Do we need a law on the confiscation of divorces – this is a means of payment from times of monarchy to 10 sizes of zinc? Or: Do we need a regulation on the correcting of the company name of Kupferbergbau Mitterberg? – You will probably give me the right, ladies and gentlemen, if I say that of course we no longer need such legislative instruments and regulations. On the content of this reform: All the simple federal laws and regulations of the federal state prior to the 1st century. January 2000 were announced and still in force as federal law, with 31. This Regulation shall enter into force on the day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. A total of around 5 000 legislation falls within the scope of the law. Of these 5 000 legislation, around 2,500 are repealed, which corresponds to about 50 percent of the adjustment rate. Of the total of around 1,650 federal laws, more than 600 will come out of effect, which is a conversion rate of about 38 percent. Of the approximately 3 350 regulations, more than 1 800 will come into force, which is an adjustment rate of around 54 percent. The legislation, which is now being deleted, has been developed and identified in a multi-stage procedure involving all stakeholders involved. In general, only constitutional laws and constitutional provisions are excluded in simple laws. We have – do you remember? – discussed in the Committee on the issue of legislative references. There is also a clarification in this regard in the materials that those references quoted in the legislation which are now being deleted remain, of course, in force for the purpose and purpose of this legislation. The benefits of this major reform – thanks to the Minister once again for having done so – are: It is obvious that it makes a big difference whether, instead of approximately 5,000 legislations available in the RIS, only 2 500, half of them, can be relevant to the solution of a specific case, a comprehensive legal clearance increases legal clarity, and finally this law creates a reduction in overregulation and red tape among which citizens, authorities and entrepreneurs are suffering. That is why I can only support this law and this project. Thank you very much.

This is the second step. The third step, for which we are all, is to look at the laws as to whether, as they exist, they are understandable, whether they are close to the citizen, whether they fulfil a purpose, whether they can be made easier. This too is a next step. But one does not make it easier for 5,000 regulations still in force to be made easier, in the fourth step, so that this and that law are no longer necessary. In other words, we are moving towards a strategy step by step: We're gonna drop what we don't need anymore. We're checking what's overful and no longer necessary, and we're making the law easier. In my view, this is a procedure that is appropriate and necessary. Mrs. Dr. Griss, you said the law doesn't make any sense at all. I mean, Professor Funk is one we all know and appreciate; in this context, he has carried out the following – I quote –: “A systematic and continuous view of the right to the same ballast is an unestimateable contribution to legal adjustment, which can significantly improve the quality of the right.” – Professor Funk is also opposed to you by also saying that it is necessary to implement this measure. There has already been a first legalisation, namely in 1999 – it has already been mentioned – which lasted 13 years, so it has certainly been carried out very elaborately. This has led to the fact that some 500 legislation has dropped out of 250, i.e. around 50 percent. And now we have implemented a legal consolidation that will result in 2 500 legislation being deleted. At that time, when it came to 250 legislation that has been abandoned, there was unanimity in the National Council, and at that time everyone felt that it was important and necessary to carry out a legal consolidation and finally to see that the law is adapted to the circumstances. In this connection, I quote Mr Jarolim, whom I very much appreciate: "For the purposes of the substance adjustment, the draft which is under discussion today provides that all the regulations which were put into force before 1946 are repealed if they are not explicitly mentioned in the annex to the law." That's exactly what we did. “It is a relatively undisputed measure, and after an extensive examination – one has to say that this project has been running since 1986 –”, i.e. 13 years, “in which the individual regulations have been reviewed for effectiveness, need, user-friendliness and manageability [...]”. Mr Jarolim, I fully agree with you. Then it goes on: “I think this project is a start. Even the date – these are legal instruments until 1946 – shows that, of course, there is still the need to continue to search through the laws [...]” – that is, laws de facto according to the 1.1.1946. That's what we did. ‘In this respect, this project is a first step [...] towards simplifying and comprehensible the law [...]’. – I agree with a hundred percent. I also appreciate it because of the statements, but not only because of them. Then it goes on to say: “I assume that we continue to work on it and, of course, we will always have to examine in the current laws these criteria according to which we have examined the laws before 1946 [...]”. - That's what we did. I therefore believe that Mr Jarolim himself and perhaps They can follow all of his judgment at the time, because nothing has changed. Changed At the time, it was only 500 laws and 250 were deleted and today we delete 2,500 laws to have a legal order that is manageable, which is easier and thus also reduces the costs of information for the legal user. I hope that the next step towards deleting unnecessary over-fulfilments of EU standards or improving the content of the laws will also be possible with your support, so that the laws in Austria can become more understandable and can be adopted by people as well. Mr Zinggl referred to the oath law. I would also like to point out that: We have done and carried out this procedure very, very conscientiously. On the one hand, each ministry in its area has reviewed all the laws that are still applicable and which are not applicable. The Constitutional Service of my home has carried out the same examination. It was then seen and summarized in a draft assessment. A six-week review followed. The working documents have also been published on the Internet, and it would have been possible, ladies and gentlemen, for you to sign up to this law that it should also be deleted. But that was not the case. That is, everyone was invited to announce which law is no longer required from his point of view – a possibility to be perceived in the future. The next step was that the feedback received under the review procedure was forwarded to the Ministries. They then examined them, as did the Constitutional Service, a communication took place – and the result is on the table today. I am sure that here is a matter on the table, which has really been very meticulously developed and brings about a corresponding improvement and more clarity. Finally, just as Mr Jarolim did in his speech at the time, I would like to thank the Constitutional Service and the Head of the Constitutional Service for the excellent preparatory work on this law, and I hope that those who have not yet been convinced will still give their approval to this proposal, because it will take a step in the right direction. Thank you.

Until then, it is possible, but after that – only because they think they can delay the procedure again – they can no longer bring in new facts, provided they have not been known before and then become known. [[17:43:59]] Member of Parliament Dr Alfred J. Noll (PILZ) : Madam President. I would have liked to congratulate the Federal Chancellor if he had two minutes left. [[Federal Chancellor Kurz leaves the meeting room.]] Without wishing to continue the debate on the urgent question, I would like to give him one way to go to Ballhausplatz: After all, he is one who quickly exceeds another border like hardly any other, and he is particularly light-footed when it comes to crossing the border of verbal suppleness to demagogy. This was a successful example today. [[Congratulations on the list of mushrooms.]] On the subject which is now being discussed in the continuation of the agenda, a few words: The European Criminal Investigation Order is a good thing. We are in favour of this being done, especially after the criticism we have put in the committee meeting, a paragraph 5 is now inserted in § 6, which also sets out the consultation mechanism that has previously been missing in the law. That's good and right. However, I believe that in the course of the parliamentary debate there is a need to point out – he does not find himself in the explanations – that the person concerned also has the opportunity to apply for a European order of inquiry if this is not done by the authority. It is important, I believe, that this specification in the Stenographical Protocol is important, otherwise it is easy to misinterpret the text of the law. Much more exciting is the revision of the administrative criminal law. We will not vote for this amendment for a number of reasons. Although it may sound like an effort to give a speech to some Members, I would like to suggest to you the three reasons why we cannot accept this amendment to the administrative penalty law. First: You know that the administrative law does not apply to the presumption of innocence. The population is hardly clear, but in administrative criminal law, the presumption of debt has so far been unfavourably valid. That is to say, the objectiveness of a violation of an administrative criminal standard is sufficient for the punishment. For many years, at the latest since we accepted and adopted the MRP as a constitutional law in Austria, the debate is being held whether or not the presumption of innocence should apply in the administrative criminal proceedings. If a first start is now set in this novel in paragraph 1a of § 5 in order to bring the presumption of innocence into administrative law, then we welcome it – but this is, please, without Courage and without heart! It is now determined that only in the case of administrative penalties over 50 000 euros the presumption of innocence should apply, for all that lies below, it should continue to remain in the presumption of debt. You can easily translate this: The large ones can rely on the presumption of innocence, they have to prove their intention in the administrative criminal proceedings, but the small ones continue to have the presumption of guilt. If one had considered this sober and had followed the voice of the heart and also the factual argumentation in the European administrative law, then one would not have written 50 000 euros, but one would have written 500 euros or 1 000 euros. This would have done most of the cases of luggage and would not have introduced a social misunderstanding again. There is also a second blatantity in this government template, which is poorly incomprehensible. If you make the government template, you will find in the explanatory notes to Article 3, that is to say, what is on § 5 para. 1a, a long-sleeping reference to the revision of § 9 para. 1 Administrative Criminal Law. § 9 regulates the responsibility of legal persons, of institutions of legal persons. The explanations refer to an amendment to § 9 which was not then made manifestly – in the explanations all of this remained so – although the amendment of § 9 would be absolutely necessary and correct in the sense of these explanations. They know that legal persons are only possible to act through their organs. The administrative criminal law imposes on the conduct of these institutions, and many of the managing directors, lawyers, department head et cetera organise the operation as it is common in large businesses. The proposed novelle insisted that an institution should not be punishable if appropriate arrangements were made to ensure that the organisation is in operation properly – a very welcome novel, if only it would have come. What this is supposed to be in the explanations now and why § 9 has not been changed in the sense of the explanations is obscene to me, no one could explain it to me. The real reason why we cannot accept this amendment to the administrative penalty law is the newly introduced § 34b under the term “identification”. So that the ÖVP has always been for more Law and Order and always for more police powers, we know. But the fact that the FPÖ is now looking so much for proof that freedom is the opposite of freedom is already amazing. What is planned in § 34b? – You know that our security bodies in the field of administrative criminal law have always had the opportunity to establish the identity of suspects when they were caught up in fresh action. That's good, that's right, that's quite true in practice. Now, in the explanations, it is stated that too many black riders in Vienna would escape the identification if one did not write the authority into the law that the identity of such persons should be determined even if, as it is stated in § 34b, “believable” such information is available. Do you know what that means? – This means that in normal cases of nocturnal rest disorder, for example, when someone calls the police and says: The neighbor was loud!, the police march comes and hears nothing at all, but then it is entitled to establish identity and, according to the provisions of the Security Police Act, to enter the apartment to determine the identity of people who have been accused. In practice, the mere knocking of charges will go exactly beyond forcing the identification of those persons by police force who in some cases have simply been approached. , and against these abuses, let's make the most of it. [[Abg. Gerstl: No!]] [[Abg. Gerstl: No!]] Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e17011	Next item is the report by Mr Stefan. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e17032	Madam President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! We are dealing here with two issues, two agenda items under one. One is the implementation of the European Investigation Order in administrative cases. It is very useful to ensure that such arrangements come from another State are: Austria must be examined by an administrative court before it is implemented. This is a very sensible arrangement. I think there's a consensus here in the house. This ensures that an order that might be valid in another State but could not be applicable in Austria is not implemented. - That's the part. The second part is the item on the agenda for an amendment to administrative criminal law. There are several points being changed, and I want to list them individually so that you can see what it is all about. One point has just been raised by my previous speaker: the so-called presumption of guilt. It is in administrative criminal law that one assumes that the person who has committed a violation is guilty, and that he himself must prove that he is innocent. Now, however, it has been found that, especially in areas where there are very high penalties that can go up to the millions, there is an indebtedness, although there is very little possibility of defence and the whole process in administrative criminal law. There are no very clear rules, as is normal criminal law. There is no such thing as this one, and I am glad to say that there is now at last a turnaround: In the case of certain offences, starting at a fine of EUR 50 000, the debt no longer applies as a presumption, but first the innocence, and the authority must prove it. Mr Noll, in principle, has considered it positive that this is now being changed, but has only meant that the amount is too high; but I think it is certainly very useful that we put it in this direction now. This is a complete reversal of administrative criminal law, even if it only applies from a certain point of view, but after all. It is, as has been said, a sensible measure. And the claim, unless socially unfair, is incomprehensible to me, because it has nothing to do with it – because of the little ones: Everyone who's a driver is a little guy, no matter what car he's got. So this has nothing to do with social matters, but it has to do with the offences that are considered there. A second very important point is the acceleration of administrative procedures. At present, it is possible for new claims to be made over and over again in the administrative procedure. That is to say, a party submits something, and it will then be ready for decision in the authority or in the administrative court, and then something new will be presented again, a new argument or a new evidence, and thus one can almost infinitely delay proceedings for a long time. Now it is made clear that the administrative authority, the administrative court, has the possibility to conclude or establish an end to the investigation procedure, and then only facts that have really come out of existence, which were created only after that, can be presented, and can no longer be artificially lengthened. This is very important for many procedures. We know this from the asylum area too. The third point is: advice instead of punishment. – It is a matter of giving authorities first advice on certain offences – but this is usefully limited – in the area where the meaning of the criminally protected property and the intensity and indebtedness are small – that is to ask for a legitimate condition, and only when it is not produced. We have experienced a great excitement in this regard, especially in the city of Vienna, which has claimed that it is now impossible to impose sanctions at all in many areas, such as gambling and so on. We have made it clear – and this fear has now also been removed – that all these administrative criminal offences are not included, for everything committed intentionally does not fall within the scope of it and is applicable only to permanent offences. For example, I can only deliberately commit gambling, so there is no advice there instead of punishing it. So this excitement was for nothing. The whole thing is now God's thanks. That's very important. Now, on the point where Mr Noll has criticised FPÖ in particular, there should be the possibility that security bodies will be able to establish identity even if they do not find those affected directly at the time of their action. For the first time, this draft law also explicitly provides for – and this is an important measure – as is done in the so-called black-and-collar process. If you were stopped in public transport without a ticket, it was not possible for the security bodies to stop the person concerned, but they had to get extra police to stop him. If this person then simply disappeared, it was no longer considered to be immediate to fresh action, but could escape, and as a result it was really a very unsatisfactory situation. That is why it has now been explicitly stated that if a person is stopped and his identity has been established, he has not committed any administrative violation at all, provided that he pays within 14 days what has been prescribed to him. That means that this is actually a decriminalization, i.e. a sensible measure, and that is why it is of course important that one can see the identity. In addition to what Mr Noll believes that there is now apparently an excretion, so that now the security bodies on call stop all possible people and their identity will be identified, one has to say clearly: The text of the law – and I may quote it so that one may know – states that the identity of a person may be established “when they enter into a fresh act or immediately thereafter either be credible in the conduct of the act or are entered with objects indicating their participation in the act”. Thus, this is so far limited: “Immediately afterward ” and“ credible. ” This has to be brought to the authority first, and the authority will, of course, consider very carefully whether it is doing so. [[President Sobotka shall take over the chairmanship.]] Should it really prove what I cannot imagine that this is going too far, then we will change it in the future. But the fact is that because of this restriction there is no danger of freedom or anything else, but – on the contrary! – with the accompanying measures, we even contribute to decriminalisation. [[Congratulations with FPÖ and ÖVP.]] Overall, these are very reasonable measures, which, on the one hand, accelerate procedures and on the other, lead to some decriminalisation. That is why I can hardly imagine anyone voting against this.

It has already been mentioned several times in the debate that this administrative procedure law, the administrative criminal law, brings a decriminalisation, including in the area of indebtedness, which was addressed by Mr Noll. Why does this bring more rule of law? – Because in the future, as has been said, the presumption of indebtedness at 50 000 euros does not apply, and I have to do so because the question has also been raised by Mr Jarolim, why just 50 000 euros: It has gone in a direction that I have taken action to ensure that more rule of law is also established in administrative criminal law, and that we have sought to take a first step towards presumption of innocence. The reason why 50 000 euros have been elected has several links. The first point of contact is the execution order, where, for example, a simplified authorisation procedure can be carried out up to EUR 50 000; in addition, the capital outflow notification law, where capital outflows amount to more than EUR 50 000; the Donation Notice Act; the Financial Penalty Act, which establishes jurisdiction at EUR 50 000. At the same time, it was in the old criminal law, where a large number of assets were linked to the amount of EUR 50 000. That is to say, we have taken 50 000 euros, taking account of these limits, and we have said that it is the first step towards decriminalisation and administrative criminal law. As a result, it can be evaluated to make a further reduction as part of the evaluation. For example, several amounts have been mentioned, 5 000 euros or the like. Therefore, it will certainly go in the direction of my department that we are expected to carry out another evaluation in two years in order to achieve decriminalisation. The second point that has been raised is “punish before punishment. ” We have also followed the best-practice experience, as, for example, is provided for in § 9 of the Labour Inspection Act, for years, where it has been established that, even in the case of injuries – in the case – workers' protection regulations are to be advised first, in the consequence, it is necessary to eliminate the illegal situation and then, as has been said, to avoid any punishment. We have also taken this path, as is necessary in the context of decriminalisation: If indebtedness is low, if there is no intentional offence, if you have not received a penalty several times in the past, in the case of “consulting before punishment”. I think that is certainly a way that is both correct and appropriate. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the FPÖ.]] With the intention of keeping it in mind at the time in § 9 of the Labour Inspection Act, one has chosen the consultation approach with the aim of: The work inspectorate should go in the direction of consulting to the necessary extent with a view to ensuring the most effective implementation of workers' protection rules. And exactly the same point is now also in place. But this requires validation, confirmation by courts. It is only possible for the courts to carry out the validation if they receive a contract under constitutional law. The administrative courts have been set up in the past as a judicial body, and this would be a further responsibility to be transferred to them. This requires a constitutional majority, and I would like to thank you for the fact that this constitutional majority is present today, which will allow the investigation order to take full place in Austria. Another point has been raised: Accelerating administrative procedures. There was also a very constructive and simultaneous use. In this respect, we have joined forces with all the national administrative courts as well as the Federal Administrative Court and the Administrative Court and have initiated this acceleration of the administrative procedure. On the one hand, there is a procedural obligation on the part of the parties and on the other hand a decision-making obligation on the part of the authorities. That is, if the authority does not decide within eight weeks, the investigation procedure, which was previously closed, will be reopened. This means that it is an incentive for the authority to act quickly. I believe that this is an approach which means that procedures that have lasted several years in the past are carried out very quickly, very targetedly and cost-effectively in this case. Another point concerning the administrative penalty procedure – we are also going towards the discharge of citizens – is that: You will know if you have received an anonymity and have paid 22 euros instead of 20 euros, for example, that it has been carried out regardless of the administrative penalty, because the amount you have committed has not been paid in time or has not been properly paid. In the future, this is different: If a citizen or a citizen has received an anonymity and has paid too much, then of course that is true. No administrative penalties shall be carried out, and the overpayment shall be reimbursed accordingly. In addition, it will also be possible in the future to withdraw an opposition made against a criminal offence. Not least, there is also the possibility that there will be a possibility for the individual types of crime, for example by the Minister of Transport, that in the future – whether it happens in Vorarlberg, in Vienna or in Styria – offences, such as the non-establishment of a belt, will be punished at the same level. ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e17878	We therefore come to the 13th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Ofenauer is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e17905	Mr President. Estimated Minister! Ladies and gentlemen here in the House! Appreciate Seers and Advisors! With this Adult Protection Adjustment Act, a variety of laws are adapted to the new Adult Protection Act, which has been adopted since 1. This Regulation shall enter into force on the day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. It is a milestone in this area because it replaces the former body and brings more autonomy, more self-determination and decision-making assistance to those concerned. The former officers are now becoming adult representatives. This is already a fundamental difference in terms of terms, because, in principle, those concerned should continue to be able to do their affairs independently as far as possible. They must, of course, be capable of action and decision-making accordingly. However, some people are not able to decide on their own, but these people should also be able to do their own business as far as possible. I am really glad – and I can thank and congratulate myself on this – that funding was also ensured by Minister of Justice Moser and Minister of Finance Löger during the spring budget negotiations. Thank you very much. I congratulate you. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] There are now four possibilities of representation: on the one hand, the precautionary power and the chosen adult representation, which are unlimited; on the other hand, the chosen adult representation must also point out that this representation can be entrusted to trusted persons from the area of the person concerned and does not have to be used as a foreigner. There will be a legal adult representation and a judicial adult representation on a temporary basis. Precautionary power – as the name says – can be given by the person concerned with caution, at a time when they are still capable of decision-making, for a time when they are no longer. This is also the case for every person in the area of the person concerned. Another possibility is the chosen representation. In this case, a person is selected only in case of need, but also by the person concerned. This takes into account the desire for self-determination and, above all, the desire of the person concerned to participate in the representation. This can also be done if decision-making capacity has already been reduced and must be established in the case of a notary, lawyer or an adult protection association. Legal representation shall end at the latest after three years. They take over the next family, and they are subject to judicial control. The judicial representation, in turn, is the previous body, which is, however, only limited to certain tasks and is no longer a representation for all matters. It also ends at the latest after three years; it is the last of all. Lawyers, notaries and so on may not take over more than 15 representations at once in the future unless they are registered in a list for particularly qualified persons. All existing authorities are to be checked by 2023 to ensure that they are still necessary to ensure that the authorities or judicial representations are used only in those cases where they are really necessary. Austria has thus recently been one of Europe's most modern representation rights, which focuses on the greatest possible self-determination and decision-making assistance for those concerned. ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e18016	Mr Deputieser Reifenberger is speaking on behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection and the Committee on the - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e18037	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen in the gallery and at home in front of the screens! ==References== July 2018, the Adult Protection Act entered into force – this is a historic step in legal dealings with so-called protected persons. The previous body was reformed and transferred to so-called adult protection law. This consists of a total of four pillars. The first pillar is the so-called precautionary authority. Since 2007, it has been established in the ABGB, but in fact there is the precautionary authority in practice for a long time. This was a development of the notaries, which have designed far-reaching powers for the purpose of preventing an instituency. The second pillar – and it is now new – is the so-called chosen adult representation. The third pillar is the so-called legal adult representation, which is a further development of the legal authority of the next member. The fourth and last pillar is the so-called judicial adult representation. This is what was once understood in about the term "scientific administration". This is the last step in this four-stage hierarchy. The judicial adult representation is, as it were, only the last remedy if none of the other three pillars can be applied. The aim and purpose of the Adult Protection Act is to strengthen the self-determination of autonomy accordingly and to reduce legal restrictions to a minimum. This is, of course, a narrow ridge, on the one hand to ensure the necessary protection, on the other hand, but not to carry out an exaggerated right. In 2017, the legislator decided to leave the pendulum in the direction of self-determination, which is the guiding principle of the adult protection law. On the part of those concerned, the desire is often expressed to have the right to make mistakes, not to be restricted by too much protection. Now we have to decide on the Adult Protection Adjustment Act, which took place on 1 January. It is intended to enter into force on August 27th and to make adjustments in a total of 27 federal laws. There are a few concerns, at least in the Committee of Dr. Griss of the NEOS, about the fact that the protection mechanisms could be reduced too much. The example that Dr. Griss referred to in the committee was that in the judicial adult representation, that is, the former board of experts, it is no longer automatically a comment in the basic book and in the company book, which was very likely the case earlier in the board. There is now only one comment if a so-called reservation of approval is expressly ordered by the Court of First Instance. I have to say that there are two heart beats in my chest. From the point of view of the founder of the contract and of course, which I myself am, I would like to see the best possible protection of the persons concerned. That is why I am able to understand the criticism of NEOS a little bit in terms of content. On the other side However, it is also true that the meaning and purpose of the law are to strengthen autonomy and to intervene as little as possible in the rights of those concerned. This is precisely what NEOS should understand; otherwise, they also attach to a very liberal image of society, and this is reflected in it in some way. However, I believe that in practical handling, the judicial system will very often make use of this reservation of judicial approval and that there will very often be comments in the basic book and in the company book. In that respect, it may be that this debate is, in fact, an academic debate. I think, however, that this law needs to be looked at after a certain period of observation, that the adult protection law may have to be evaluated, and then perhaps at one point or another a little bit of an intensification. Until then, we look forward to more self-determination on the one hand and less prejudice on the other – truly a liberal principle.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e19126	Next, Mrs Lintl will speak. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e19148	Madam President. Madam President-in-Office of the Council! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! The Court of Auditors reviewed the so-called Austrian Higher Education Area for the period 2010 to 2015. This includes all tertiary education institutions. These include 21 public universities, the Danube University Krems, twelve private universities, 21 universities and 14 educational universities. This concerns, and this is particularly interesting, Ladies and gentlemen, almost 380 000 students throughout Austria. In particular, the legal framework and the higher education strategy of the then competent Federal Ministry of Science, Research and Economics, the Austrian Higher Education Plan and the financial framework from 2010 to 2015 were reviewed. The Austrian university room was compared to that of Switzerland. I would like to draw some points from this report. The Court of Auditors notes that competence for universities was divided into several, namely three different Federal Ministries. This was inconspicuous and fragmented, it does not have a single line in the Austrian high school system. There was a lack of a uniform approach in the financial field. In Switzerland, however, federal and cantons were jointly responsible, which is a huge advantage. The new government has changed that. With the new Federal Ministry of Education, Science and Research, a Ministry of Education is now at last responsible for the whole field of education; from kindergarten to all schools, universities and research institutions, a minister is responsible. [[Congratulations with the FPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP]] Here too, the government creates very clear structures, greater transparency and clarity for the benefit of all. The Court also recommended a clear definition of the functions of the advisory bodies. The Science Council advises the Minister of Science, and the Council for Research and Technology Development advises the entire government. The Court of Auditors therefore recommends that these two bodies should be grouped together. The demand for a single advisory body of the Federal Government has already been included in the government programme and will also be complemented by a national economic competence. The aim of the Austrian Higher Education Plan is to ensure the highest quality in teaching and research. Austrian universities are to be made more visible internationally. For excellent research for universities and non-university institutions, incentives are provided and competition is promoted. Overall, the new Federal Government implements the recommendations of the Court of Auditors, including in terms of higher education plan, higher education strategy and funding. Thank you very much for this report, Madam President.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e20337	Madam President. High house! The examination of day care in schools analysed the development in a field of activity that is developing dynamically, both at the level of the Ministry of Education and in two countries, namely in Salzburg and in the city of Vienna. We have described that the situation in the field of school day care is complex and unclear. Why is that so? – Because day care was originally not covered by the school’s task. The day care of students is characterized by a variety of legal regulations, actors and organizational forms. Depending on the type of school, whether it is federal school – in that case we have examined AHS sub-levels – or general compulsory school, there are different responsibilities in relation to school maintenance and staff; once it is teaching staff, then it is leisure education and educators. Building requirements, group sizes and support contributions are regulated differently. In principle, out-of-school day care is only possible in the area of out-of-school day care, i.e. at the hortens. In this sense, I believe that one will not miss out on thinking about what belongs to a school today – in the morning, in the afternoon, in the age of digitization, migration or inclusion. What is pedagogy and what school maintenance? It is only when you clarify this question that you will know who is to carry out the different tasks in relation to school meaningfully: Federal, Länder or Municipalities. So what were the objectives of day care in school? – There was a second article-15a agreement in 2013, in which the federal government and countries set themselves the goal of extending some 119 000 places in public and private schools for day care to 200 000 places by the school year 2018/19 and creating a 30 percent care density. There were also 50,000 extra-school places for out-of-school day care. This corresponded, for example, to the need planning. However, some 19 000 places of lunch care were added to the AHS sub-levels. This was just a supervision and was not considered to be school day care. In principle, we have found that midday care has, so to speak, undermined the use of school day care especially in AHS sub-levels and, above all, in the agglomeration centre. There was a supply density of about one third in the city of Vienna; there was less demand in Salzburg, but there was an increase of around 49 percent – above the Austrian average. We have spoken about the target subsidies that have been made: It was EUR 654 million planned. There was a very strong federal policy force for this school day care; the federal government is only responsible for the AHS sub-levels, the countries and also the municipalities are responsible for the rest of the compulsory schools, and therefore the federal government takes over the initial funding. There was no provision in the Article-15a agreement for non-consumable funds, and our recommendation was to make funds available to countries only for consumption. In this case, it With the Education Investment Act, there was a change, so that 15a agreements are no longer necessary. It has already been discussed that there are different valuation contributions in terms of tariffs, which are mainly in the care area; in Salzburg there were 137,80 euros in compulsory schooling, which is 40 percent less than in the Hort in the city of Vienna. We are in the first week of vacation and it is always about the holiday care of the students, but because this does not fall into the area of school, this also does not fall under school day care. In the AHS area, there was no replacement service, and in principle it depends on the school holders whether they are willing to provide a holiday care. In the Ministry of Education, there were objectives of effect, and the measure of extending school day care was also defined as a contribution to raising the level of education and improving equal opportunities and gender equality, but there were no empirically secured confirmations. It is a fact that pupils and children and young people from disadvantaged groups have made less use of the offers. In the case of a school-care service, there is still a lack of the regulation that teachers can take on further activities. As far as school psychology is concerned, I assume that school psychology is a federal matter and social work is a national matter. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e20602	We now come to points 18 to 23 of the agenda. There was no oral reporting. Mr Singer is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e20629	Madam President. Madam President of the Court of Auditors! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) The President of the Court of Auditors has already announced that we are dealing with a lot of reports today, one of them dealing with the transport service contracts on the rails. Why Transport Services Contracts? – The Federal Government and the countries order train kilometres through transport service contracts because the services provided are not covered by collective income and can only be provided economically through public co-financing. In short, for transport service contracts, routes that cannot be managed cost-effectively by the charges of passengers are subsidized. Only to have an order of magnitude: For 2014, the Court of Auditors determined 91.8 million train kilometres with a volume of EUR 870 million. What were the criticisms of the Court of Auditors? – The Federal Government and the Länder built parallel structures for the award and settlement of the transport service contracts. This means that trains ordered by different local authorities went on the same routes at different times. The Court of Auditors said that the use of synergies via joint and central resolution bodies would be necessary. A conflict of interest has also been identified: As a customer, the Federal Government and the Länder clearly are interested in buying the services as cheaply as possible. However, as the owners of the essential railway undertakings, they are also interested in ensuring that they are managed without losses. This tension ratio meant that the financing of public transport and the economic situation of railway undertakings were not transparent. As a further point, it was pointed out that the Federal Ministry of Transport, Innovation and Technology, when ordering rail-person transport services at ÖBB, exceeded the budget provided for in the Federal estimates by an average of around 27 million euros per year or 4.7 percent. The Court of Auditors recommends that, in accordance with the budgetary principles of completeness and truth, all the expenditure expected in the following financial year should be included in the estimates. Ladies and gentlemen! This issue concerns very, very much money, taxpayer's money. The rail system is an essential part of public transport in our country, and that is a good thing. However, as in all areas, we must also ensure that tax money is as efficient as possible. It's going to be handled. It is therefore necessary to pay particular attention to the Court's proposals by all audited bodies. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e20769	Madam President. Madam President-in-Office of the Council! High house! Ladies and gentlemen in the gallery and at home in front of the screens! Natural disasters, as evidenced by numerous events in recent years, will be an ever-increasing threat to mankind – whether storms, floods, snow, hail, heat waves or earthquakes. Preventive measures are becoming increasingly important. In the coming years, the Austrian population has to adapt to increasingly frequent, non-predictable extreme weather events. As a result, disaster aid is becoming increasingly important. Ladies and gentlemen! The Court of Auditors reviewed in April the conduct of the Federal Ministry of Finance and the countries of Lower Austria, Salzburg and Tyrol in connection with disaster aid. The period under review essentially covered the years 2011 to 2015. It was found that the aid paid by the countries in the event of disasters was highly different. In Lower Austria, the aid amounted to on average 24 percent of the recognised level of damage. In Tyrol, on average, 37 percent and in Salzburg, on average, 61 percent of the recognised amount of damage was paid out. In the future, however, disaster aid will be managed in such a way as to ensure equal treatment of comparable damage events. This ensures that our citizens are equally supported in the face of such severe disasters. Mein Ladies and gentlemen! At this point I would like to thank all the professional and voluntary institutions as well as our Austrian Federal Army. All of you are providing great help in the event of disaster. I would particularly like to highlight the good work done by our Minister for Defence Mario Kunasekher, because he will make it possible for the German army to go even faster in the face of regional disasters. [[Congratulations with the FPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Thus, our population can rely on our federal army in the event of disaster. Thank you.

I would like to highlight a more detailed report, which is about EU funds from the point of view of impact orientation, in order to promote structural policy in the EU. As a summary of the report, one can say: Too many developers, too many institutions are busy and there is too little communication, too little effectiveness. The EU financial framework 2014 to 2020 for promoting the implementation of structural policy in the EU has a volume of EUR 371 billion, which is available for this purpose. We in Austria receive a little more than 1.2 billion euros over this period. From the Court's point of view, the number of objectives, together with the number of bodies involved in the funding process, is increasing the cost of the programme. That is what the Court of Auditors report says, that is, as much as: Too many people are busy and there are too many goals. From 2007 to 2013, 37 institutions were busy distributing this funding. There were also 16 other authorities involved. In the meantime, it is only more than 16 developers, as the Court of Auditors report says, but this is also a little too much for the Court of Auditors, especially since the promoters communicate too little with each other. This is also the final record of this report. The result is of course: too little effectiveness. This is quite clear when 16 authorities are busy distributing the money. I think it's clear to everyone that this can't be very effective – similar to our social security schemes, of which there are too many. The Court's recommendation to the Federal Chancellor's Office in its role as overall coordinator for the EU Structural Funds in Austria would be to work towards adequate concentration of objectives for the next programming period at the European Commission. That is to say, we should choose fewer targets and really promote them effectively. In calculating the financial impact of projects in the context of impact-oriented impact assessments, the effort would be fully reflected in relation to the local authorities involved. ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e21308	Mr President. Madam President of the Court of Auditors! Dear ladies and gentlemen! High house! In 2010, a transparency database was established in Austria. It should recognise double funding and ensure that tax money is used safely. Overall, this audit of the Court of Auditors, which was carried out between April 2016 and July 2016, assessed the years 2010 to 2016 and highlighted the potential benefits of the transparency database for the purpose of transparency control function and prevention of funding abuse. It was essentially about the fact that the federal, state and local authorities were affected and there was a so-called funding jungle, which was found by the Court of Auditors. A total of 22 recommendations were made by the Court of Auditors in relation to these six years, with costs of EUR 13.6 million at that time. Overall, the problem was that the competence for the transparency database was both in the federal and in the countries. The lack of user-friendliness was also criticized by the Court of Auditors. By 2016, the transparency database included 2 426 services and made publicly available, and there were payments of a total of 208 billion. the euro. According to the Court of Auditors, a complicated instrument has been created with the transparency database. The objectives of this transparency database could not be achieved. A total of three major points can be taken out: It is a question of exploiting the potential of these, as I believe, very useful and very beneficial institutions for the state. It is a question of being able to record national benefits and municipal benefits in the same way as federal benefits. It is a question of involving community associations and chambers in the same way in the services in order to ensure continuous control. It is also about improving access in terms of promotion. It is therefore a rather critical report as a whole, but there is also a welcome communication: The 2017 Court of Auditors activity report noted that the transparency database in Austria is now being strengthened and better adopted. So there are now developments that are quite pleasing. Countries have agreed to feed their data. The pioneer is the Federal State of Upper Austria, but also Lower Austria has now promised that it will provide the data on its funding. A level which is very critical is the community level. The community has expressed its concerns, because it is also a question of the extent to which, from what size and amount of funding should be provided at all. In summary, you can say: This Court of Auditors report is a clear task to give greater transparency and insight in this regard. The Court of Auditors has announced that it will also make a follow-up review and will note further developments. Thank you.

Whoever has read the OECD press release today will be able to see that it will also refer to the Gender Pay Gap in connection with the dampened wage growth we have had in Austria in recent years, but in particular since the fourth quarter of 2016. One cause is the relatively low productivity of the country – there is a need for re-examination – and the other is the employment of women in the low-paid sector. ParlaMint-AT_2018-07-04-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00034_d8e21862	Mr President. Madam President-in-Office of the Council! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! The monopoly management society is not only responsible for the retailing of tobacco products, but also has a social policy task of enabling as many privileged people as possible – people with disabilities – to become an economic existence as a tobacco traficient. This has historical reasons. Already Emperor Joseph II. The tobacco monopoly was adopted in 1784. From the beginning, war invalids, soldiers' widows, and innocently impoverished officials were assigned Trafiantenstelles so that they could take care of their own income. This was particularly important after the wars in order to enable war - injured people a secure existence. Even today, people with disabilities are preferred in awarding, and the Trafikantenstelles are awarded on a lifetime basis. However, members also have a legal right to enter and continue the tobacco traffic. The Court of Auditors recommended restricting this right in favour of people with disabilities, because two thirds of the tobacco shops awarded were then passed on by relatives, which is not the idea behind the matter. The management of the monopoly management shall endeavour to comply with this recommendation. This is not so easy, however, because trafiques with revenues below 80 000 euros per year are not profitable, which is why there is little interest in such calls. That's why it's important to have the free-handed possibility of awarding. There are fewer and less tobacco bars. We have already heard that 853 trophies were closed in the test period. But this is also clear, because fewer and fewer cigarettes and fewer newspapers and magazines – which are replaced by online media – are sold, and stamps, postcards and postcards, all of which are no longer up-to-date. However, the monopoly management society also has a social policy mission. Their aim is to keep the number of tobacco shops stable. There should be a healthy structure of economically successful trafiks that meet the needs and are real specialist businesses. Another criticism of the Court of Auditors was the protection of young people. Traficants are legally obliged to check the age of consumers. There too, the monopoly management society has acted and focused on the field of youth protection. To ensure that the legal situation is secured, all contracts – including those that have passed for a long time – have been renewed and the possibility of examination has been established. The monopoly management company, together with the Trafiken, is also seeking a uniform solution to the issue of financial revenue when it takes over Trafiken, which the Court of Auditors has also referred to. In total, it should be noted that Monopolverwaltung GmbH has received and implemented the Court of Auditors’ recommendations. Thank you very much for the report, Madam President.
